# Claim ledger: limits L1 to L3 (round 1 blind audit, 26 Nov 2026)

Scope: `app/content/courses/limits/` at commit `4f60ebdc`: `l1` to `l12`, `review-1` to `review-3`, `homework-1` to `homework-3`, `problems-1` to `problems-3`, and the six fluency sets of these levels (`fluency-l2`, `-l7`, `-l9`, `-l10`, `-l11`, `-l12`). Stage 1, full ledger depth. The course has no code widgets.

Every row was read and judged by hand. The rows were then laid out by a script from the content, so that no string is skipped: one row for each prose block, each graded key, each wrong option with its `why`, the slips of a widget, the hints of a screen (grouped), the feedback lines of a screen (grouped), each `explain`, each summary line, each model answer and rubric. A grouped row is `correct` only if every line in it is.

Paths are shortened: `s3` is `screens[3]`, `q1` is `quiz[1]`, `i4` is `items[4]`, `i2.p1` is `items[2].parts[1]`, `F0` is `families[0]`; `w` is `widget`, `o2` is `options[2]` with its `why`, `fb` is `feedback` (correct and byKey), `ex` is `explain.text`.

Basis: "script" names a check in `docs/reports/limits-L1-3-concepts.py` (sympy 1.14, exact arithmetic where possible), which recomputes every graded key, every slip value and every stated number from the screen's own function. "By hand" means a short derivation done while reading. Sources named: Stewart, *Calculus* (8th ed.), by section; Spivak, *Calculus* (4th ed.), ch. 5 (limits), 6 (continuous functions), 9 (derivatives), 10 (differentiation), 12 (inverse functions); Apostol, *Calculus* vol. I; Hull, *Options, Futures and Other Derivatives* (9th ed.), ch. 4 (bond pricing, duration) and ch. 19 (delta). Rows marked F1 to F12 are the findings of `docs/reviews/audit-limits-1-3.md`.

Rows: 1656. Verdicts: correct 1634, imprecise 19, misleading 2, wrong 1.
